i would like to thank the commissioner for her reply and ask a question arising from it. in light of the recent investigation by the commission into the arrangements of ryanair at charleroi airport, is it not now the case that regional airports in europe are unsure how state aid rules are going to apply in the future and that this in itself is going to impinge on legitimate commercial decisions they would like to take, as well as on their future development?
mr president, i was interested to hear commissioner byrne say that some of the findings exceed the limits. this is because, according to press reports when this scientific study was first published, a statement was issued claiming that a spokesperson from commissioner byrne's office had agreed with the assessment made by the united kingdom food standards agency that the levels of chemicals in our european salmon did not exceed eu limits. i welcome the fact that this position has now been revised, because what we are talking about here is about consumers. background levels have to be taken into account. the issue needs to be addressed as to why those background levels are there in the first place. that is something that also has to be tackled. from these studies it has been quite clear that farmed salmon have a significantly higher contaminant burden than wild salmon – that is what the report says – and that farmed salmon from europe are significantly more contaminated than farmed salmon from south and north america. a lot of people have long seen that there is obviously some sort of health risk in relation to farmed fish. we are now saying that consumption of farmed salmon should be limited to a certain amount per week, or per month, which raises a question about the threat to consumers. it should be clearly labelled so that consumers know what they are eating and have that choice. i raised the issue with the president of parliament, pat cox, and asked him to request the european food safety authority to issue a scientific opinion on the problem. he agreed to do so. unfortunately, we have still not received the letter which he was going to send. it is crucial that this should not be further delayed, because we are not just talking about salmon here. an issue which has not been raised at all is that other carnivorous fish species, for example tuna, cod, sea bass, sea bream and trout, are farmed in similar conditions and probably exposed to similar contamination. this is an issue which needs to be addressed, not just in relation to farmed salmon but in relation to other farmed fish. finally, i see that the united kingdom government has adopted protection principles to safeguard scottish salmon. they should adopt protection methods to safeguard the consumer.
mr president, let me say unequivocally that if there are proven problems of a serious health or environmental nature linked to eu-produced salmon, then the matter must be dealt with seriously and swiftly. however, there must be accurate, reliable and totally independent scientific advice before any such action can be undertaken. it will not surprise the commission that i am somewhat sceptical, to say the least, in relation to some scientific studies currently doing the rounds and which, i believe, prompted the present crisis. i am not interested in who did the study. i am, however, interested in who paid for the study. i am also interested to know if there are vested interests involved in this work, if there is anyone who stands to gain from this type of so-called scientific research. for me, the crisis in the salmon sector is not just environmental; the real crisis is that the eu salmon industry, particularly in my constituency of connaught-ulster in the west of ireland, is under serious threat of extinction. the entire community industry is under threat from cheap imports, or to put it another way, from dumping of farmed salmon from norway, chile and the faroe islands. it is totally unacceptable to me that a growing number of independent irish and scottish producers are going into receivership. if this continues unchecked, there will be no environmental or health issues to resolve because there will be no eu salmon. in recent days, both the irish and uk governments made a joint application to the european commission for new measures to safeguard our salmon-farming industry. the application does not concern the canadian or the american producers; it concerns the eu producers, starting with irish producers who are fighting for survival. in conclusion, i ask the commission to tell me and the irish salmon producers, whom i represent, what follow-up or initiative it has taken to date, or intends to take, on the joint irish-united kingdom application?
president of the republic of colombia. (es) mr president, members of the conference of presidents, members of the bureau of parliament, committee chairmen, members of the european parliament, ladies and gentlemen: many people who visit colombia often say that it is a wonderful country, in stark contrast to the image of analysts and the news. others say that it is an illegitimate, authoritarian and elitist state. with the strengthening of the state, our pluralistic democracy has made the transition from formal and rhetorical guarantees to effective and real ones. the referendum proposed by the government and voted on on 25 october was discussed and approved by the congress, modified by the opposition, examined and reduced by the constitutional court. abstentionists and opponents had every opportunity to express their views. no one could complain of curtailed freedoms or government hostility. the next day, the people elected 30 governors from among 133 candidates; 914 mayors from among 3,441 candidates; 398 deputies from among 373 lists comprised of 2,723 candidates. the number of candidates doubled compared to previous competitions. in several cities and departments candidates from groups that are alternative to the traditional parties, from organizations derived from former guerrillas, from groups calling themselves left or opposition, have won. in the campaign they launched, the government's main concern was to provide them with full guarantees. and now that they are elected, the government's decision is to work with them, within the constitution, without considering the political origin of their election, with transparency, and above all, with the patriotic design of uniting colombia within diversity. popularly elected mayors and governors have been victims of terrorist pressure and assassination. at the beginning of this government, 416 mayors were threatened by the guerrillas and 216 could not attend to their responsibilities in the territory of their jurisdiction. the advance work on security has allowed the problem to persist in only 14 localities. colombia has discussed for more than a century the popular election of mayors, as an essential step of democratic enlargement. the terrorist guerrillas demanded it as a condition of peace. it was passed in the '80s. but here are the paradoxes of terrorism: it is the executioner who murders the local democracy he claimed. our state is one of mutually controlled institutions and broad public opinion participation, which subjects citizens to the rule of law and not to the whim of the authoritarian ruler. the security concept of the government i preside over is democratic, to protect all citizens above their ideas, their economic situation, their status as entrepreneurs or workers, their affinity or disagreement with the government. dealing with 30,000 terrorists is no easy task. we are aware that we have the challenge of overcoming the terrorist challenge with transparency, as a budget to build national unity and give sustainability to democratic security, which seeks to restore law and order. while terrorism is the great violator of human rights, the state and the police follow them with devotion. last year, 76,864 members of the institutional forces were trained in human rights. in 2002, the ombudsman's office received 231 complaints against the police for abuses of international humanitarian law. the number dropped to 160 in 2003. the control bodies, the judiciary and the government share the determination to punish severely any proven violation of human rights. colombia thanks and praises the courage of its soldiers and police. their sacrifice to give us peace of mind has been immense: since august 2003, 277 soldiers and police have been killed by antipersonnel mines alone, and 854 have been injured, many of them permanently maimed. our country is open to the surveillance of the international community. we have the presence, as requested by us, of the office of the united nations high commissioner for human rights. their recommendations are received with respect. when they're not amenable to government judgment, we discuss them frankly. i recognize the space of human rights ngos, i value the work of many, without prejudice to expressing my disagreement with reports that do not correspond to the facts. in almost 1,200,000 square kilometres, with more than 400,000 hectares of forest, the presence of the state has been lacking for years, supplied by terrorist organizations. we have set out to expand the institutional capacity to protect citizens. of the country's 1,100 municipalities, 170 had no police presence. today it's on everyone, though we won't rest until every last urban center is protected. in 450 municipalities we have installed soldiers and marines, called "de mi pueblo", who serve in the locality where they reside. the results are good, and there is great hope that after their active period in the public force, they will be leaders in respecting the law, and for ever. between 2002 and 2003, homicides decreased by 20 percent, kidnappings by 26 percent, massacres by 33 percent, attacks on the population by 84 percent. in january 2004, there were 51 kidnappings compared to 186 in january 2003, and homicides decreased by another 17%. the murder of teachers, trade unionists and journalists has decreased and, fortunately, the killers in several of the cases are already in prison, as a consequence of our determination to defeat impunity. i reaffirm our commitment to protect teachers, trade unionists and journalists, because no democracy can allow them to be killed. the violent deaths of indigenous people, indigenous cooperators and environmentalists, cruelly persecuted by terrorist groups, have decreased due to the presence of the state in ancestral territories. internal displacement is one of our great tragedies stemming from terrorism. in the last year it decreased by 53% and 11,140 families have returned to their places of origin. while we have made progress, the task is just beginning. what country can settle for 2,200 kidnappings in a year? we will only sing victory when massacres and acts of terror are completely eradicated, because each one that happens brings pain to the entire nation, like the car bomb that was detonated by the farc at club el nogal, exactly one year ago, where 33 people were killed and 173 were injured; or the farmer from la unión peneya, who was burned alive by the terrorist guerrillas because he refused to leave his home and go to add to the number of displaced people; or the woman from tibú, who lost her leg to an explosive mine while walking with her 5-year-old daughter. the constitutional reform that introduces anti-terrorism standards was passed by congress amidst national and international debate. the powers granted to the public force to capture, intercept and search in cases of terrorism were subject to the subsequent approval of a statutory law, and also to other guarantees such as the obligation to hand over the case to justice in the following 36 hours, the immediate report to the prosecutor's office and the periodic control of congress. the rule is less strong than those in many western democracies. i think the world should reconsider the notion that these kinds of powers given to governments put human rights at risk, because they require equal and full observance by both the judiciary and the executive. i classify violent groups as terrorists because of the nature of their actions, the democratic context of colombia, the international comparative legislation, the source of their resources and the result of their activity. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. anyone who acts against democracy, through violent action, in the midst of the colombian determination to give guarantees to all expressions of political thought, can only be called a terrorist. the legislation of many countries defines a terrorist as one who, for ideological or other reasons, commits or threatens violence. similarly, anyone who acts in this way in colombia can only be called a terrorist. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat the spread of the disease, including the introduction of a new vaccine, the introduction of a new vaccine and the introduction of a new vaccine. with a cessation of hostilities as a requirement, although disarmament and demobilization require long periods. colombia has had a generous tradition of negotiated settlement with violent groups. my predecessor, president andrés pastrana, made a huge bet to negotiate with the farc. the good faith and patriotism of that president were cowardly cheated by the farc, who grew their criminal empire while the nation waited and cherished the hope of the peace agreement. i have given priority to democratic security because i believe that it will make the violent understand the need to negotiate seriously, otherwise they will be totally defeated by the institutions. however, efforts have been made to initiate negotiations with the violent ones. with the help of the catholic church in cuba, with the intervention of the group of friendly countries and the facilitating commission, we have sought dialogue with the eln. so far no results despite our willingness. they are afraid to take any step in favor of peace without permission from the farc. i received a mediation process from the catholic church with illegal self-defenses and decided to continue it. i agreed to the peace commissioner initiating the dialogue when these organizations announced the cessation of hostilities. it is time to improve and monitor the cessation of hostilities, and for this the support of the organization of american states is most useful, which must lead to a rapid concentration of those members of these groups who have a sincere desire for peace. the legal order is equally attacked when the state and society are violently attacked, as when it is pretended to defend them by appealing to violence and to private justice groups. we want to restore the principle of institutional protection exclusively for citizens. the demobilization of the self-defence forces contributes to this. the government's military action has been relentless against the self-defence sectors that have not respected the cessation of hostilities. this is evidenced by the number of discards, which increased by 85% in 2003 compared with 2002, and the number of catches, which increased by 135% over the same period. the widely discussed criminal alternative bill seeks to facilitate peace agreements, without impunity, in harmony with justice. although its procedure coincides with the process that is being advanced with the self-defence, it is mostly needed for the guerrilla, which is superior in members and registers the largest number of demobilized in the course of the current government. in that law we must look to the past and anticipate the future. the past's pardons for heinous crimes are unacceptable today and so would it be if tomorrow the law we pass was changed, depending on the political predisposition towards other terror actors who want to negotiate. during the government's term, almost 4,500 members of violent groups have been demobilized, 68% of them members of the farc guerrillas. they have been welcomed with great generosity and the state is making great efforts to help them reintegrate into society. this number exceeds what has been achieved in peace processes in recent decades. the farc has more than 1,000 hostages in their custody, including congressmen, members of the public force and former presidential candidate ingrid betancur, who is a colombian and french citizen. i understand the pain of their families and the international interest in a humanitarian settlement. i initially conditioned it on the cessation of hostilities and the initiation of dialogue. my talks with the church, the french government, the un secretary-general kofi annan and the families of the victims led me to accept the pursuit of the agreement on the basis of a negotiation conducted through the un, the release of all the hostages, and the commitment of the terrorist guerrillas who leave prison not to commit further crimes, with effective guarantees, such as their residence in a country that could protect the agreement. without the effective guarantee that they will not re-offend, the government cannot release terrorist guerrillas. otherwise, it would be to abuse the sacrifice of the public force, to demotivate its action and to wither the popular spirit of respecting the law. it would open the door to a recurrent extortion of the state and society. we must bear in mind that some are good citizens kidnapped by terrorism and others are terrorists who are in prison. we must bear in mind that war is one thing and terrorism another. in war there are contenders who are recognized as legitimate for the purpose of humanitarian agreements, whereas in terrorism there is an executioner and a victim who is society, whose rights and virtues we must protect with the force of institutional arms. that is why the authorities cannot renounce the rescue of the abducted, despite the cruelty of the executioner, demonstrated in the murders of gilberto echeverri, guillermo gaviria, doris gil, helmut bickenback, chikao muramatsu, and a large number of members of the public force. murders of defenseless citizens committed by the farc when they presumed the presence of authority in the vicinity of the captivity. no negotiation can make the executioner stronger. colombia is a social democracy. 63% of the nation's budget, excluding debt service, is allocated to social protection, education and health, directly and through decentralization. terrorism has made poverty worse. peacebuilding requires that we overcome that poverty. the government is working on a plan called "the 7 tools of equity", which are: the educational revolution, social protection, the promotion of the solidarity economy, the social management of the countryside, public services, the country of owners and the quality of urban life. coverage of basic and secondary education reached 86.3%; the state technical education service will achieve its target of increasing from 1,070,000 students per year to 4,000,000 in the next four years; the various child nutrition programmes benefit 5,000,000 children; credit for small businesses grew by 57% in the last year. we have many social problems, but we are all determined to overcome them. the economy grew last year between 3.7% and 4%, when 2% was expected. in the last year the unemployment rate fell by 3 points and that scourge of unemployment stood at 12.3%. in colombia, inequality is not due to private enterprise but to the lack of private enterprise. the organised private enterprise has high burdens to contribute to solidarity with its workers and the rest of the population. our employers pay most of the parafiscal obligations, and the latest tax reforms have increased their taxes. terrorism has slowed the expansion of private enterprise and this has increased informality with misery. we enthusiastically promote worker enterprises, cooperatives and the participation of users and workers in the provision of public services. we believe in social capitalism. to combat the concentration of rural property in the narcotics trade and related terrorism, we passed a law to speed up the procedures for extinguishing domain. in the last year, we went from five convictions to 61. the european union has given us invaluable support, and i want to thank you. the london declaration commits us to a deep commitment. it has been a great source of democratic commitment, a great framework of european support. the funding of the peace labs is welcomed by the community. we need more cooperation to defeat the drug. without drugs, there would be no terrorism. it is not the time to blame some for producing it and others for consuming it. the problem is so great that any permissive country ends up being a producer, a trafficker and a consumer. it's time to beat the drug. our farmers who grow it feel cheated by the terrorists and affected in their dignity. they long for alternatives. we already have 20,000 ranger families to look after the coca-free areas and oversee the recovery of the forest. each one gets about $2,000 a year. we need to reach 50,000 families. young europeans should know that drugs have destroyed 1,700,000 hectares of rainforest in colombia, just at the entrance to the amazon basin, which is seriously threatened by this scourge. in the andean community we are concerned that the european union is not taking the decision to negotiate a free trade agreement with our countries, when it has perfected it with mexico and chile, and is proposing to bring it forward with mercosur. the imperfections of andean integration which are alleged to prevent the negotiation of this treaty would be overcome by the obligations imposed by the agreement with the european union. we welcome the extension of the gsp and respectfully request that it be maintained until it is replaced by a permanent agreement. i come to this parliament with deep respect, with gratitude for the interest shown and the continuing support for colombia. i am aware that the solutions my government is seeking to the complex problems facing my country are controversial. i reiterate my faith in creative deliberation. it builds more deliberation than absence. it builds debate more than retreat. it builds more constructive controversy than the abandonment of the curriculum. you know my intransigence in the face of terrorism. my generation has not lived a day of peace. the country we want for our children and those who will come after us must be a country of peace and justice. i would ask you, distinguished members of the european parliament, to accept my reiteration of my deep love for democracy. (applause from the right)

mr president, firstly i would like to welcome to this house the irish minister for justice and home affairs. secondly, i would like to offer my sincere congratulations to my colleague and friend, mr ribeiro e castro, for the presentation he made here this morning. the european union is soon to become a political body comprising 25 member states with a population of nearly 500 million people. it is therefore of the utmost importance that all member states in the european union cooperate more closely to combat the threat of international terrorism and organised crime. with common european borders in existence and with the free movement of people within the union, we too must cooperate more closely in an effort to stop drug imports into europe and to combat the evil of human trafficking. we cannot defeat drug trafficking and those who engage in the heinous crime of human trafficking unless our european police forces work closely together. this challenge becomes even greater as enlargement takes place, and europol will have to play a more pivotal role in coordinating the european union's response to these rising crimes. no country can defeat international terrorism on its own. if europe requires more resources to face up to this new challenge, then more resources must be made available. in recent times we have seen the tragic deaths of many people who have fallen foul of the scourge of international human trafficking. it is estimated that 600 000 illegal immigrants enter the european union every year. that is why a comprehensive plan to combat illegal immigration must remain a key priority of our union. eu financial assistance for the return action programme must be maintained and we must all support the work of the union. the borders agency is putting in place a coordinated approach by eu member states to combat illegal immigration. i too support the implementation of a common eu asylum policy, and we must resolve the outstanding issues concerning asylum qualifications and procedures directives. we must also support the better management of the entry of persons into the european union who are in need of international protection in accordance with the geneva convention. with regard to the issue of legal migration, the new eu legislation governing the conditions of entry and residence of third country nationals for the purposes of study and vocational training within the union must be promoted. finally, the issues of putting in place common policies to combat illegal immigration are best addressed at european union level. we now live in an internal market where there is free movement of persons across the territories of the eu, and we must take collective action together in an effort to deal with these issues effectively.
mr president, i would also like to welcome minister mcdowell to the house. i would like to pick up on a couple of small points, given that this is the end of the debate and many of the points i wished to raise have already been dealt with. referring to mr evans' reference to legal migration, which i totally agree with, i am very proud and happy that ireland is one of only two countries in europe not to have raised barriers to the movement of workers from some of the new applicant states coming to work in our country, as many of them are already doing. while listening to the commissioner and to the minister listing the legislation that has been brought in, particularly in the area of immigration and asylum, i know both men have warm hearts, but in the description of all the legislation that was coming through, the quality of mercy seemed to be lacking in some way. the contribution that the visitors who have come to live amongst us have made to life in many of our countries must be recognised and celebrated. indeed, minister, when i think of this i think of my hairdresser, in your own constituency. the quality of service and gaiety and everything else in our little hairdresser's, where we middle-aged ladies go to have our hair done, is considerably enhanced by the young chinese students who are working there; we all queue up to have them wash our hair. while that might seem trivial in the context of the many serious issues here today, it is something we must celebrate. it is not all a negative criminal issue; it is also a considerable contribution to the quality of life. mention was made, particularly by the commissioner, to some of the civil judicial areas that have come through in the last year, many of which contribute considerably to the quality of life of our citizens. my own area of particular interest is the protection of children, as well as the question under the brussels ii convention of access to and custody of children in parental disputes. i know it is not done in this house very often, but i cannot but refer to the difficulties i am having with the federal republic of germany in many cases, particularly relating to fathers having access to their children, who have either gone legally or illegally back to live in germany. we have situations where, for example, it appears to be perfectly easy and legal for a custodial parent in germany to change the name of the children – the children very often bearing the father's name – without any reference to the father. names are being changed legally, which causes considerable distress to the fathers, who in many cases are also denied access to their children, both legally and through the social services. unfortunately, despite great efforts by myself and many others, these fathers are finding it extremely difficult to gain access to their children. while the commissioner thinks that this kind of legislation is not often covered by the press, i have to say that this particular issue is indeed covered by the press; it is something that people genuinely care about and there is an element of justice that has to be recognised in this. commissioner, finally, could you please look into your heart and see if you could not do anything to improve our relationships with the non-hague convention countries, to which many children are still being abducted?
mr president, the third non-proliferation treaty prepcom in new york in a few months' time has to be a success. to that end, it is essential that ireland, as the holder of the presidency, ensures that member states adopt a common position that will become part of its commitment to the eu strategy against the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction adopted in thessaloniki, because these are the ultimate weapons of mass destruction. ireland, as one of the instigators of the non-proliferation treaty, has a major role to play here. we would like to know, in relation to the council working group on nuclear weapons, what the priorities are for the npt prepcom meeting. parliament needs to know which member states are being cooperative and which are not. the council should prepare regular progress reports on this issue for parliament, in particular on the issues of the dismantling of nuclear weapons arsenals, nuclear weapon-free zones and the no first-strike option. this is in the public interest. we need to know how far eu member states have implemented the action programme of the 13 practical steps agreed in 2002 at the npt review conference. it is also very important that the irish presidency takes a proactive role to ensure that europe becomes a nuclear-free zone, that the uk and france get rid of their nuclear arsenals and that nato's first-strike policy is abolished. the council has to prepare a statement on the progress made by the eu since the report in 1995 by this parliament on the npt and, indeed, the many resolutions since then. i would like to see a proactive position from the irish presidency, informing this parliament about what is happening within the council.

mr president, the israeli prime minister ariel sharon claims that the construction of his country's controversial wall is to protect its citizens from terrorist attacks. i abhor terrorism and would support the right of any government to take appropriate measures to defend the lives of its citizens. however, the strategy of building this wall is morally wrong, as well as being counter-productive. like many of mr sharon's other counter-terrorist strategies, this fails to tackle the root causes of terrorism. palestinian land has been confiscated to build the barrier; thousands of palestinian farmers and traders are cut off from their land and means of economic survival. if the wall is completed, it is estimated that approximately 380 000 palestinians will be trapped between the wall and israel's 1967 pre-occupation border, the so-called 'green line'. furthermore, 160 000 of these would be trapped in 'double walled' ghettos or enclaves. palestinian communities would be effectively isolated from one another. they would also be cut off, in certain instances, from centres such as jerusalem, which have been the focus of their economic, social and religious life. recent proposed modifications will not prevent this. furthermore, the restrictions on the freedom of movement of goods and services will intensify the problems for an already weakened palestinian economy. the consequences of this ill-conceived security strategy on the part of the israeli government will provide a fertile breeding ground for creating another generation of terrorists rather than effectively tackling the problem. the issue of the legality of the wall has now moved centre stage. the refusal of israel to comply with the resolution adopted at the october general assembly of the united nations to stop and reverse the construction of the wall led to the general assembly decision of 8 december to refer the matter to the international court of justice. israel should pause and reflect on the consequences of its actions. the wall violates the fourth geneva convention, specifically in the two areas of prohibition of the use of collective punishment and prohibition of annexation, which cannot be abrogated by invoking military necessity. it also violates the oslo agreement in respect of the obligation to preserve the territorial integrity of the occupied palestinian territories and the prohibition against restricting freedom of movement. when one also considers the continued refusal by israel to comply with un resolutions, it begs the question: 'where does israel think it is going?'. does it want to be a pariah state, regarded in the same light as rogue states that are in constant defiance of their international obligations? or instead, will it work with the international community to create peace in the middle east? with progress on the so-called roadmap slow or virtually non-existent, one has to question the merit of imposing such a controversial measure on the palestinian people. if the israelis are serious about making real progress in the peace process and putting the 'roadmap' back on track, the building work on the security wall should not only stop, it should be reversed. the israelis and the palestinians should instead be working together to take down barriers and to build confidence between the two communities if an agreed settlement is ever to be reached. the recent declaration by sharon that he plans to dismantle most of the 21 jewish settlements in the gaza strip is a step in the right direction. however, building yet more security walls and crippling the already impoverished palestinian economy serves only to make it more difficult to achieve peace in the middle east.
mr president, i hardly dared to think that i would get such a long and comprehensive answer to what was in fact a three-line question. it was really a trap for the minister because naturally i am interested in what is happening throughout europe, but i am particularly interested in what is happening within my own constituency, where there is considerable concern that the irish government is in fact not fulfilling previous promises to various groups that have put considerable effort into devising programmes and projects under this heading. i refer particularly to dun laoghaire, rathdown, which in many cases is recognised as being an affluent area but does, of course, have serious pockets of disadvantage, which local organisations have made a significant effort to address. i am very glad to hear that the irish government and presidency are as committed as the minister has said. minister, what are you going to do closer to home to ensure that many of these projects that are extremely well thought-out are actually going to survive?

with such very kind comments from the president-in-office i am almost sorry that i have announced my retirement from the european parliament. as we have seen with the recent outbreak of avian flu in thailand, the question of country of origin is one of genuine concern to european consumers. if proper traceability exists, then every food outlet should know the country of origin of the meat and other food products it serves. i know, and i am sure the minister knows, that producers and consumers in my own country, ireland, are concerned that many restaurants are not able to provide this information. apart from the safety and quality issues, this denies the consumers the right to support home or european production as opposed to third-country production. my supplementary question is: does the council accept that consumers had valid concerns in this area? i know now that it does and i am grateful to the president-in-office for acknowledging this fact. what further action can the presidency take to ensure that this issue is addressed in future proposals on labelling? i know the council will have a very positive input into the eventual outcome of this vital area of consumer concern.

in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because i am in favour of extending the powers of the european maritime safety agency to maritime safety and the training of seafarers. parliament is right to stress the need to make maritime transport safer, to protect the marine environment and to improve safety at sea. in the wake of the prestige disaster and the many other disasters we have witnessed, we all agree that maritime transport must be made much safer and that there must be much better coordination of action in all the member states. a properly funded european maritime safety agency would allow such coordination, while fully respecting the need to respect the principle of subsidiarity and recognising that the main responsibility must lie with the coastal member states. i also fully support the proposal that the european maritime safety agency should be involved in the recognition of seafarers' qualifications and i hope that the agency will give priority to this particular area in order to create greater safety for workers and citizens. i don't think the agency should be in charge of counterterrorism. it is up to the member states to do so, and i welcome the clarifications in the report on this point.
in writing. - i would like to confirm my support for this report on the authorization of austria and luxembourg to sign the 2003 protocol before 30 june 2004. i believe that in the wake of the prestige accident there is a clear need to ensure full and prompt compensation for the victims of future oil spills, regardless of our commitment to do everything in our power to prevent such disasters from happening in the future. i call on all member states to sign, ratify or accede to the protocol as soon as possible.
in writing. - i would support this call for the commission and the member states to learn from europe's biggest corporate scandal for several years and to strengthen the eu's audit rules, especially in the interests of workers. in the wake of the accounting scandal surrounding the italian food group parmalat, which broke in december 2003 with the disclosure of a black hole of eur 4 billion in lost capital and eur 14 billion in outstanding debts, i believe it is absolutely necessary for the commission to draw up a revised directive on corporate governance with stricter audit rules. on several occasions, workers and pensioners have been the losers in corporate scandals, so there can be no doubt that there is an urgent need to improve corporate governance and strengthen our measures against corporate dishonesty. good corporate governance and good corporate governance are essential for the security of employment of all workers and for the protection of future members of occupational pension schemes. the financial services sector must be cleaned up to ensure that it is ethical and prudent in protecting the jobs and savings of millions of people. it is scandalous that no alarm bells were sounded when parmalat's funds were misappropriated. - the next item is the communication from the commission on the commission's annual policy strategy for 2005.
vice-president of the commission. (es) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the annual political strategy adopted this morning by the commission, which i have the honour to present, involves, firstly, setting out the political priorities for 2005 and the initiatives that will help to implement them and, secondly, presenting the budgetary guidelines so that the priority initiatives receive the necessary resources. the second point will be raised by my colleague, mrs schreyer, so i shall concentrate on the first part. as we have done every year for the past two years, we have been in a structured dialogue with the european parliament and the council. in view of the specific features of 2004, the structured dialogue will take the form of a meeting between the commission and the conference of commission presidents during the month of april. the conference of presidents will then take stock of the situation at a second meeting in september. we are aware that we are in a year of transition in which our institutions will pass the baton to their successors; and this is precisely what makes dialogue of key importance, since we have the fundamental role of ensuring the continuity of the functioning of our institutions as well as possible. this year is special in that it is addressed not only to the budgetary authority but also to the next commission. it is important to ensure the continuity and consistency of the commission's policies and to ensure that we fulfil our institutional obligations, and that is what we are trying to do. our objectives are therefore the following: first, to ensure continuity, that is, to hand the baton to the next commission which will have to confirm or possibly modify, when it starts its work, the priorities we have presented here today. secondly, to prepare the ground for the incoming commission to preserve this margin of manoeuvre in political and budgetary terms. thirdly, to ensure consistency with the strategic guidelines already established, especially the financial perspective. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, 2005 will be the first full year of a commission with 25 members, of a union with 25 members, of a new commission and a new parliament. the new commission will therefore have to start implementing a political agenda that corresponds to its mandate. the commission will also be presenting a report on the implementation of the new financial perspective. the numerous meetings scheduled should take account of very important issues such as the mid-term review of the lisbon strategy, the review of the sustainable development strategy (sustainable development and therefore new measures to adjust and improve its implementation), the definition of a new social agenda, the consolidation of the european area of security, freedom and justice and the conclusion of the accession negotiations for romania and bulgaria. before the end of this year we will have to submit the report on turkey and therefore in 2005 we will eventually see the next steps. at international level, 2005 should also remain largely geared to issues of international security and stability, the fight against terrorism and the reconstruction of iraq. the main objective is to implement enlargement properly, ensuring its success; to shape the future europe, to shape what already exists, ensuring the proper functioning of the enlarged europe and the full implementation of policies and rules in all the union's states, both new and old. in this connection, we have three priorities: first, competitiveness and cohesion; second, security and european citizenship; and third, external responsibility, partnership and neighbourhood. in the first place, given the poor economic performance of the european union as a whole in recent times, the persistent stagnation of our productivity and employment rates and our desire to achieve a fully successful integration of the new member states, the revival of growth should be the main political objective, by continuing to seek to improve the competitiveness of the european model, preserving prosperity and cohesion, without sacrificing key elements such as sustainable development. the second priority is security and european citizenship. it should be remembered that the enlargement of the union's territory and the increase in its population will require greater efforts and more coordination because of the management of new common borders, the increased mobility of people in this area and the need to ensure their security. the union will have to manage a land border of more than 6,000 km and a sea border of more than 85,000 km, given that the total number of third-country nationals residing in the union is well over 15 million and annual arrivals are around 1.5 million. enlargement raises the question of the diversity of cultures and peoples and presents a new challenge: european citizenship as a new dimension of the union. the third priority, mr president, is the european union in the world, external responsibility and hence, in particular, partnership and neighbourhood. the new dimension of the union, the new external borders resulting from enlargement, make it necessary to establish a stable and comprehensive political framework with our neighbours to the south and east. the commission will therefore implement a new neighbourhood policy for the union for those states which do not wish to become members, that is to say, to join it. the commission will also focus its external action on the following objectives: to contribute to the completion of the enlargement process under way, which means preparing the accession of romania and bulgaria by 2007; and, referring to the decisions taken in 2004, the commission will pursue the pre-accession strategy with turkey and could be in the active negotiating phase with croatia if things go well. as i said at the beginning of my speech, my colleague mrs schreyer will give the details on the question of human and financial resources, but let me say that in these two areas the situation is critical and delicate as she will say in a moment. the commission's request is the minimum necessary to enable the institution to meet its institutional responsibilities in the future, taking account of enlargement and, above all, taking account of the new policies which are the subject of community attention. i hope that, within the framework of the extraordinary interinstitutional cooperation which characterizes the budgetary process and with the understanding of the european parliament, appropriate responses can be given to these challenges. (applause) i would like to thank
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. the vice-president of the commission outlined the commission's priorities for 2005. the priorities are to be equipped with the necessary means to meet the objectives and expectations of european policy. the year 2005 will be the second year in which we have a budget for a european union of 25 member states, and i very much hope that we will vote on this budget for a european union which will include all the citizens of cyprus at first reading. the financial perspective, which we adjusted last year to take account of enlargement, sets the financial framework for the next financial year. the adjustment was made in accordance with the copenhagen decisions and the amendments made by the european parliament. the copenhagen decisions, which are enshrined in the treaties of accession, set out the financial commitments to the new member states. the council also decided to increase the preaccession aid for bulgaria, romania and turkey. the change in the 2005 budget compared with this year is thus clearly marked by enlargement. the enlargement of the union is a great political, economic and environmental achievement, but it also presents a challenge for the new member states, who must implement all eu laws, and also for the european commission, which, as guardian of the treaties, must ensure that these laws are implemented and which must work intensively in the coming years to ensure that integration succeeds. the commission's staff must be adapted to this end. the commission presented the overall development plan in 2002, with the result that a total of 3 900 additional posts are needed. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would also like to take this opportunity to express my sincere thanks to the budgetary authority and, in particular, to parliament, as my colleague, mrs loyola de palacio, has done. you have given the commission a great deal of support over the past few years and we are counting on your support to enable the new commission to meet its institutional requirements and tasks. the financial perspective for 2005 provides for a budget of eur 114 billion. the ceilings for financial commitments in internal and external policies and administrative expenditure are lower than those envisaged in the programming, because the adjustment of prices to exchange rate changes is less pronounced. this will make the scope for action next year extremely narrow. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the resources for new initiatives in internal and external policies are very limited. however, the commission is proposing new initiatives and increased funding for 2005 in line with the priorities, for example in the field of transport and energy for the preparation of a european air traffic management system, in the field of industrial policy for the establishment of the agency for the testing of chemicals, in the field of justice and home affairs for the further development of the schengen information system, and in research more funding is needed for the decontamination of nuclear research facilities. these are just a few examples. in foreign policy, commitments must be respected in all areas, from commitments to combat aids to measures in kosovo to cooperation with non-governmental organisations to strengthen democracy. we are currently expecting that financial commitment to iraq will be necessary in 2005. so we will again have the situation where we have to resort to the flexibility instrument in order to be able to fulfil the tasks which the european union has undertaken. the discussions on the 2005 budget will take place in a new context or with a new composition for all the institutions, as the vice-president has already explained. it will be new for the council to adopt the budget with 25 members. the parliament will be a newly elected parliament at the first reading, but it is very good that mr garriga polledo has been appointed as a main rapporteur, who is very experienced and will ensure continuity in the discussions. the commission will alternate between the first and second readings of the 2005 budget. the budgetary procedure in 2005 will have many new faces in purely institutional terms. on 16 march 2004, we will discuss the priorities for the 2005 budget in the trilogue, and i am confident that as we have done well in recent years agreement on the priorities can be reached quickly so that a good european budget can be adopted again for 2005.
